3) Blocks setup — reply (the if/else chain): procedure reply message → set local messageLower to call TextBoxAsk.Text [use the to lower case text block on message] → if messageLower contains "hello" or messageLower contains "hi" then set LabelChat.Text to join LabelChat.Text + "\nBot: Hi! How can I help?" else if messageLower contains "your name" then … + "\nBot: I am Gin's chatbot." else if messageLower contains "bye" then … + "\nBot: Goodbye! Come back soon." else … + "\nBot: I do not understand that yet — try hello.".

Test checklist: Each keyword gives its own reply; an unknown word gives "I do not understand" (not a wrong confident answer, not a crash). Chat history grows correctly, newest at the bottom. Common bug: "Hello" with a capital H does not match — fix: the to lower case block in step 3, and use contains instead of = so "hello there" also matches. Success check: Gin's chatbot answers at least 4 keywords correctly and handles unknown words honestly. Homework: Add 3 new keywords + replies of his own (e.g. "thank you" → "You are welcome!"). Needs / fallback: Needs no device, account, model or internet — rule-based blocks only. If the Companion is unavailable, the whole session works in the Blocks editor + on paper.

To get more resistance!", "I would tell you an Ohm's Law joke, but it is too revolting."), unknownReplies (list: "I do not know that one yet.", "Hmm, try asking about hello or a joke.", "Not sure — can you say it another way?"). Common bug: random always picks the first item — fix: check the random integer from 1 to length of list block uses the list length (not a fixed 1), and that the list actually has 3 items. Success check: Gin's chatbot gives varied replies from lists, picked at random. Homework: Add a third keyword list of his own (e.g. "bye" with 3 different farewells). Needs / fallback: Needs no device, account, model or internet — lists + random blocks only. Fallback if random feels too unpredictable for a demo: add a counter so replies rotate in order (1st, 2nd, 3rd, then back to 1st) instead of random — same lists, different picker.

Session 81Smart quiz — quiz that adapts: harder after 3 correctWed 31 May 2028
Wed 31 May 2028 | Phase 6 — AI & Smart Apps

Date: Wed 31 May 2028 | Phase: Phase 6 — AI & Smart Apps Learning objective: Gin can build a quiz that gets harder after 3 correct answers in a row and easier after a wrong answer. (Exploration — adaptive logic in blocks, not a real AI model.) Components: LabelTitle, Text "Smart Quiz"; LabelLevel, Text "Level: Easy", FontSize 16; LabelQuestion, Text "Tap Start", FontSize 18; ButtonA, Text "A", FontSize 16; ButtonB, Text "B", FontSize 16; ButtonC, Text "C", FontSize 16; ButtonD, Text "D", FontSize 16; LabelScore, Text "Score: 0 | Streak: 0", FontSize 16; LabelFeedback, Text ""; ButtonStart, Text "Start Quiz", FontSize 18; global variables easyQuestions, hardQuestions (lists of question sub-lists, see step 3), score, streak, level (text, "Easy"), currentQ (number, 1). Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: add the labels, four answer buttons and Start button above; set Screen1 Title to "Smart Quiz".

2) Blocks setup — question lists: initialize global easyQuestions to create empty list with 3 easy items, each a sub-list [question, A, B, C, D, correct letter] — e.g. ["What is V when I = 2 A and R = 10 Ω?", "5 V", "10 V", "20 V", "40 V", "C"]; initialize global hardQuestions with 3 harder items — e.g. ["Two resistors in parallel, 10 Ω and 10 Ω. Total?", "5 Ω", "10 Ω", "20 Ω", "0 Ω", "A"] and ["P = V × I. V = 10 V, I = 2 A. P = ?", "5 W", "12 W", "20 W", "200 W", "C"].

3) Blocks setup — show: procedure showQuestion → set local listToUse to if global level = "Easy" then global easyQuestions else global hardQuestions → set local q to select list item listToUse global currentQ → set LabelQuestion.Text to item 1 of local q → set ButtonA.Text to item 2, ButtonB.Text to item 3, ButtonC.Text to item 4, ButtonD.Text to item 5.

4) Blocks setup — answer + adapt: procedure checkAnswer letter → set local q to [current question as in step 3] → if letter = item 6 of local q then set global score to global score + 1 → set global streak to global streak + 1 → set LabelFeedback.Text to "Correct!" → if global streak >= 3 and global level = "Easy" then set global level to "Hard" → set LabelLevel.Text to "Level: Hard" → set global streak to 0 → set LabelFeedback.Text to join LabelFeedback.Text + " Level up — harder questions now!" else set global streak to 0 → set global level to "Easy" → set LabelLevel.Text to "Level: Easy" → set LabelFeedback.Text to "Not quite — back to Easy." → set LabelScore.Text to join "Score: " + global score + " | Streak: " + global streak → set global currentQ to global currentQ + 1 → if global currentQ > length of list [current list] then set global currentQ to 1 → call procedure showQuestion.

5) Blocks setup — start: when ButtonStart.Click → set global score to 0 → set global streak to 0 → set global level to "Easy" → set global currentQ to 1 → set LabelLevel.Text to "Level: Easy" → call procedure showQuestion; wire ButtonA–D to call checkAnswer "A" etc.

6) Test: answer 3 easy questions correctly → level must change to Hard; answer one hard question wrong → level drops back to Easy.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — write 3 easy + 3 hard questions on paper; 10–20 demo — Rica plays a quiz that levels up; 20–65 build — lists + show first, adapt logic second; 65–80 test & fix — play two full rounds (one all-correct, one with a wrong answer); 80–90 share — Gin plays one round for Rica and explains the 3-in-a-row rule. Test checklist: 3 correct in a row → "Level: Hard" and harder questions appear. A wrong answer → back to "Level: Easy". Score and streak update every answer. Common bug: level never changes — fix: check the if global streak >= 3 block is inside the correct-answer branch (not the wrong branch), and that streak resets to 0 on level-up. Success check: Gin's quiz changes difficulty by itself based on his streak. Homework: Write 3 more hard questions (with answers) to add to the hard list. Needs / fallback: Needs no device, account, model or internet — lists + if/else blocks only. If time is short, use 2 questions per level instead of 3 (keep the 3-correct rule, with the list wrapping round).

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — draw the flow: photo → shake → result; 10–20 demo — Rica shakes and a result appears; 20–65 build — photo first, shake trigger second, result third; 65–80 test & fix — classify 4 objects by shaking, tune the shake threshold (12) up/down if it triggers too easily or never; 80–90 share — Gin demos shake-to-classify to Rica with a resistor photo. Common bug: the classifier runs many times per shake — fix: the alreadyClassified flag in step 3 (set true before classifying, reset false on a new photo); also raise the threshold from 12 to 15 if normal walking triggers it. Success check: Gin's app classifies only after a shake, and shows the result. Homework: Change the trigger to a tilt (e.g. tilt phone left, xAccel < −5) and test whether it still works. Needs / fallback: Needs the trained classifier model, a camera, and an accelerometer (most phones have one — check with the live X/Y/Z display). Fallback: if the classifier model will not load, replace Classify with the rule-based guesser from Session 73 and keep the shake trigger — the sensor + trigger logic is still learned. If the phone has no accelerometer, use a Button "Classify" as the trigger instead and note the swap.

Common bug: history disappears after reopening — fix: check step 2 loads from TinyDB on Initialize with the exact tag "classifierHistory", and that step 4 stores the whole list (not just the latest entry). Success check: Gin's classifier history survives closing and reopening the app. Homework: Classify 3 more objects at home and check they are in the history tomorrow. Needs / fallback: Needs the trained classifier model and a camera. Session 86AI ethics & limits — when the classifier is wrong, why? (discussion + test)Wed 5 Jul 2028
Wed 5 Jul 2028 | Phase 6 — AI & Smart Apps

Date: Wed 5 Jul 2028 | Phase: Phase 6 — AI & Smart Apps Learning objective: Gin can test his classifier on tricky photos, explain why it got some wrong (bad training data, similar objects, poor light), and say when not to trust it. (Exploration — discussion + test, no new model.) Components: LabelTitle, Text "When Is the Classifier Wrong?"; LabelQuestion, Text "Test photo: —"; ImageTest, Picture "", Width 250, Height 200; ButtonTestA, Text "Test: Resistor in the Dark", FontSize 16; ButtonTestB, Text "Test: Wire Next to Resistor", FontSize 16; ButtonTestC, Text "Test: Blurry Photo", FontSize 16; LabelGuess, Text "Guess: —", FontSize 16; LabelRightWrong, Text "Was it right?"; ButtonRight, Text "✓ Right", FontSize 16; ButtonWrong, Text "✗ Wrong", FontSize 16; LabelWhy, Text "Why it went wrong:"; TextBoxWhy, Hint "e.g. too dark, looks like a wire"; ButtonSaveWhy, Text "Save Reason", FontSize 16; Camera1; PersonalImageClassifier1; TinyDB1 (tag "classifierMistakes"); global variables correct, wrong (numbers, 0). Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: add the labels, Image, three test buttons, Right/Wrong buttons, TextBoxWhy and Save button; set Screen1 Title to "AI Limits"; load the trained model from Sessions 74–76.

2) Blocks setup — tricky tests: when ButtonTestA.Click → set LabelQuestion.Text to "Test photo: resistor in the dark — take the photo now" → call Camera1.TakePicture; same for TestB ("wire next to a resistor") and TestC ("blurry photo — move the phone while taking it"). when Camera1.AfterPicture image → set ImageTest.Picture to image → call PersonalImageClassifier1.Classify image.

3) Blocks setup — result + count: when PersonalImageClassifier1.ClassificationComplete result → set LabelGuess.Text to join "Guess: " + result → set LabelRightWrong.Text to "Was it right? Tap ✓ or ✗"; when ButtonRight.Click → set global correct to global correct + 1 → set LabelRightWrong.Text to join "Right: " + global correct + " | Wrong: " + global wrong; when ButtonWrong.Click → set global wrong to global wrong + 1 → [same update] → set LabelWhy.Text to "Why? Type a reason and tap Save Reason".

4) Blocks setup — save the reason: when ButtonSaveWhy.Click → if TextBoxWhy.Text = "" then set LabelWhy.Text to "Type a reason first" else call TinyDB1.StoreValue tag "classifierMistakes" valueToStore join [old value + "\n• " + LabelGuess.Text + " — " + TextBoxWhy.Text] → set TextBoxWhy.Text to "" → set LabelWhy.Text to "Saved. Try the next tricky photo.".

5) Test + discuss (the main work): run all three tricky tests, count Right/Wrong, and for each Wrong write a reason — then discuss with Rica: (a) the classifier only knows what it was trained on; (b) dark/blurry/similar objects confuse it; (c) never trust it for safety, money, or identifying a person — a wrong resistor guess is harmless, a wrong medical/safety guess is not.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — predict which tricky photo will fool it most; 10–20 demo/discussion — Rica shows one wrong guess and asks why; 20–65 build + test — the test buttons first, then all three tricky photos; 65–80 test & fix — count Right/Wrong, save a reason for every Wrong; 80–90 share — Gin presents his Right/Wrong count and his best explanation of why the classifier failed. Test checklist: All three tricky photos tested with a Right/Wrong tap and a saved reason for each Wrong. The app never claims a guess is certain — it shows a guess, and Gin judges it. Common bug: Gin marks a wrong guess as Right because he wants a good score — fix: Rica checks one photo herself; the honest count matters more than a high count, and a low score here is a good finding. Success check: Gin can explain, with his own test evidence, two reasons a classifier goes wrong and one situation where it must not be trusted. Homework: Find one more tricky photo at home (e.g. an object half-hidden) and test it. Needs / fallback: Needs the trained model and a camera. Fallback: if the model will not load, run the same three tricky tests on the pre-made model from Session 80 (or the rule-based guesser) and record the same Right/Wrong + reason — the lesson is about limits, not about which model fails.

Session 87Smart app 1: resistor identifier — photo / classifier + decoder combinedWed 12 Jul 2028
Wed 12 Jul 2028 | Phase 6 — AI & Smart Apps

Date: Wed 12 Jul 2028 | Phase: Phase 6 — AI & Smart Apps Learning objective: Gin can combine his classifier (photo → "resistor") with his colour-code decoder (colours → value) in one resistor identifier app. (Exploration.) Components: LabelTitle, Text "Resistor Identifier"; ImagePhoto, Picture "", Width 250, Height 180; ButtonTakePhoto, Text "Take Photo", FontSize 18; ButtonIdentify, Text "Identify", FontSize 18; LabelClassifier, Text "Classifier says: —", FontSize 16; LabelBand1, Text "Band 1:"; SpinnerBand1, ElementsFromString "Black, Brown, Red, Orange, Yellow, Green, Blue, Violet, Grey, White"; LabelBand2, Text "Band 2:"; SpinnerBand2 (same colour list); LabelBand3, Text "Band 3 (multiplier):"; SpinnerBand3 (same colour list); ButtonDecode, Text "Decode Value", FontSize 18; LabelValue, Text "Value: —", FontSize 20; Camera1; PersonalImageClassifier1; global variable lastImage (text, ""). Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: add the Image, photo/identify buttons, classifier label, three colour Spinners with their labels, Decode button and value label; set Screen1 Title to "Resistor Identifier". (Colour values from Session 4 / his 6 Nov 2026 report: black 0, brown 1, red 2, orange 3, yellow 4, green 5, blue 6, violet 7, grey 8, white 9.)

2) Blocks setup — photo + classify: when ButtonTakePhoto.Click → call Camera1.TakePicture; when Camera1.AfterPicture image → set ImagePhoto.Picture to image → set global lastImage to image; when ButtonIdentify.Click → if global lastImage = "" then set LabelClassifier.Text to "Take a photo first" else call PersonalImageClassifier1.Classify global lastImage; when PersonalImageClassifier1.ClassificationComplete result → set LabelClassifier.Text to join "Classifier says: " + result → if result ≠ "Resistor" then set LabelClassifier.Text to join LabelClassifier.Text + " — check it really is a resistor before decoding!".

3) Blocks setup — decode (from Session 4): when ButtonDecode.Click → set local d1 to [index of SpinnerBand1.Selection in the colour list minus 1] → set local d2 to [same for SpinnerBand2] → set local mult to [10 to the power of (index of SpinnerBand3.Selection minus 1)] → set local value to (d1 × 10 + d2) × mult → set LabelValue.Text to join "Value: " + local value + " Ω". Example: brown (1), black (0), red (×100) → (10 + 0) × 100 = 1,000 Ω.

4) Test: photograph a real resistor, tap Identify (expect "Resistor"), set the Spinners to its real bands, tap Decode, and check the value against the resistor's known value (or Rica's check).

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — draw the two halves: photo half + decoder half; 10–20 demo — Rica identifies one resistor end to end; 20–65 build — classify half first, decoder half second; 65–80 test & fix — test 3 real resistors, compare decoded values; 80–90 share — Gin identifies one resistor for Rica, photo to value. Test checklist: Photo → classifier says "Resistor" (or warns if not). Spinners set to brown/black/red → "Value: 1000 Ω". Wrong bands → a wrong value, honestly shown (the decoder trusts Gin's band reading). Common bug: multiplier wrong by ×10 — fix: check the power: red as multiplier is ×100 (10²), not ×10; test with brown/black/red = 1,000 Ω and brown/black/brown = 100 Ω. Success check: Gin identifies a real resistor by photo and decodes its value in one app. Homework: Identify 3 resistors at home (photo + bands + decoded value) and write the values down. Needs / fallback: Needs the trained classifier model, a camera, and real resistors to photograph. Fallback: if the classifier will not load, skip the Identify step and use the decoder half only, with a label "Classifier not available today — decoder only" — do not invent a classifier result. The decoder half alone still counts as the session's core skill.

Session 88Smart app 2: circuit helper — chatbot + calculator combinedWed 19 Jul 2028
Wed 19 Jul 2028 | Phase 6 — AI & Smart Apps

Date: Wed 19 Jul 2028 | Phase: Phase 6 — AI & Smart Apps Learning objective: Gin can combine his electronics chatbot (Session 79) with his circuit calculators (Sessions 91–92 logic, built here in simple form) in one circuit helper app. (Exploration.) Components: LabelTitle, Text "Circuit Helper"; LabelChat, Text "Bot: Ask me about circuits!", FontSize 16, Height 200; TextBoxAsk, Hint "e.g. series total?"; ButtonSend, Text "Send", FontSize 16; TextBoxR1, Hint "Resistor 1 (Ω), e.g. 10"; TextBoxR2, Hint "Resistor 2 (Ω), e.g. 20"; ButtonSeries, Text "Series Total", FontSize 16; ButtonParallel, Text "Parallel Total", FontSize 16; LabelCalc, Text "Total: —", FontSize 18; TextBoxV, Hint "Voltage V"; TextBoxR, Hint "Total R (Ω)"; ButtonCurrent, Text "Current I = V ÷ R", FontSize 16; LabelCurrent, Text "Current: —", FontSize 16. Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: add the chat labels/buttons at the top, then the R1/R2 TextBoxes with Series/Parallel buttons and LabelCalc, then the V/R TextBoxes with the Current button and LabelCurrent; set Screen1 Title to "Circuit Helper".

2) Blocks setup — chatbot: reuse Session 79 procedure reply — keywords "ohm", "series" ("Series total: add them. 10 Ω + 20 Ω = 30 Ω."), "parallel" ("Parallel: 10 Ω + 10 Ω = 5 Ω total."), "power" ("P = V × I"), plus unknown → random honest reply from the Session 78 list. Wire when ButtonSend.Click as in Session 77.

3) Blocks setup — series: when ButtonSeries.Click → if TextBoxR1.Text = "" or TextBoxR2.Text = "" then set LabelCalc.Text to "Type both resistors first" else set LabelCalc.Text to join "Series total: " + ((TextBoxR1.Text as number) + (TextBoxR2.Text as number)) + " Ω" — check: 10 + 20 = 30 Ω.

4) Blocks setup — parallel: when ButtonParallel.Click → [same empty check] else set local r1 to TextBoxR1.Text as number → set local r2 to TextBoxR2.Text as number → set local total to 1 ÷ ((1 ÷ local r1) + (1 ÷ local r2)) → set LabelCalc.Text to join "Parallel total: " + local total + " Ω" — check: 10 and 10 → 5 Ω; 10 and 20 → 6.67 Ω (round to 2 decimal places with the format block if shown).

5) Blocks setup — current: when ButtonCurrent.Click → if TextBoxV.Text = "" or TextBoxR.Text = "" then set LabelCurrent.Text to "Type V and R first" else set LabelCurrent.Text to join "Current: " + ((TextBoxV.Text as number) ÷ (TextBoxR.Text as number)) + " A" — check: V = 20, R = 10 → 2 A. Also copy the total into TextBoxR automatically after a Series/Parallel tap, so Gin does not retype it.

6) Test: ask the bot "series total?", then tap Series Total with 10 + 20 → 30 Ω; tap Parallel with 10 + 10 → 5 Ω; calculate current with V = 20, R = 10 → 2 A.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — draw the chat half and calculator half; 10–20 demo — Rica asks the bot then calculates a total; 20–65 build — chatbot first, series/parallel second, current third; 65–80 test & fix — test all three calculations with two sets of values each; 80–90 share — Gin demos the full helper: one bot question + one series + one parallel calculation. Test checklist: Bot answers series/parallel/power correctly. Series 10 + 20 = 30 Ω; parallel 10 + 10 = 5 Ω; current 20 ÷ 10 = 2 A. Common bug: parallel gives 0.2 or 20 instead of 5 — fix: check the brackets — it must be 1 ÷ ((1 ÷ R1) + (1 ÷ R2)), not (1 ÷ R1) + (1 ÷ R2) alone; and check text was converted to numbers. Success check: Gin's helper answers a circuit question and calculates series, parallel and current correctly. Homework: Use the helper to solve: R1 = 20 Ω, R2 = 20 Ω in parallel, V = 10 V — what is the total and the current? (Answers: 10 Ω, 1 A.) Needs / fallback: Needs no device, account, model or internet — chatbot rules + maths blocks only. If time is short, build series + current and leave parallel as homework (its formula is practised again in Session 92).

Session 91Series circuit calculator — total resistance, current, voltage dropWed 9 Aug 2028
Wed 9 Aug 2028 | Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools

Date: Wed 9 Aug 2028 | Phase: Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools Learning objective: Gin can build a series circuit calculator that gives total resistance, current, and each resistor's voltage drop from V, R1 and R2. Components: LabelTitle, Text "Series Circuit Calculator"; TextBoxV, Hint "Supply voltage V, e.g. 20"; TextBoxR1, Hint "Resistor 1 (Ω), e.g. 10"; TextBoxR2, Hint "Resistor 2 (Ω), e.g. 20"; ButtonCalc, Text "Calculate Series Circuit", FontSize 18; LabelTotal, Text "Total R: —", FontSize 16; LabelCurrent, Text "Current I: —", FontSize 16; LabelDrop1, Text "Voltage drop R1: —", FontSize 16; LabelDrop2, Text "Voltage drop R2: —", FontSize 16; LabelCheck, Text "Check: drops add up to V"; ButtonClear, Text "Clear", FontSize 16. Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: add the three TextBoxes, Calculate/Clear buttons and four result labels above; set Screen1 Title to "Series Calculator".

2) Blocks setup — calculate: when ButtonCalc.Click → if TextBoxV.Text = "" or TextBoxR1.Text = "" or TextBoxR2.Text = "" then set LabelTotal.Text to "Type V, R1 and R2 first" else set local totalR to (TextBoxR1.Text as number) + (TextBoxR2.Text as number) → set local current to (TextBoxV.Text as number) ÷ local totalR → set LabelTotal.Text to join "Total R: " + local totalR + " Ω" → set LabelCurrent.Text to join "Current I: " + local current + " A" → set LabelDrop1.Text to join "Voltage drop R1: " + (local current × (TextBoxR1.Text as number)) + " V" → set LabelDrop2.Text to join "Voltage drop R2: " + (local current × (TextBoxR2.Text as number)) + " V".

3) Blocks setup — check: after step 2, set LabelCheck.Text to join "Check: " + (drop1 + drop2) + " V — should equal " + TextBoxV.Text + " V" — compute drop1 + drop2 from the same local values, not by reading the labels back. Example: V = 20, R1 = 10, R2 = 20 → total 30 Ω, I = 0.67 A, drop1 = 6.67 V, drop2 = 13.33 V, check = 20 V.

4) Blocks setup — clear: when ButtonClear.Click → set TextBoxV.Text to "" → set TextBoxR1.Text to "" → set TextBoxR2.Text to "" → reset all four result labels to "—" → set LabelCheck.Text to "Check: drops add up to V".

5) Test: V = 20, R1 = 10, R2 = 20 → check the four numbers above (allow rounding to 2 decimal places); then V = 12, R1 = 10, R2 = 10 → total 20 Ω, I = 0.6 A, drops 6 V + 6 V = 12 V.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — draw a series circuit (battery, R1, R2 in a line) and label what the app must show; 10–20 demo — Rica works one example on paper; 20–65 build — total + current first, voltage drops second, check third; 65–80 test & fix — test both examples, fix rounding; 80–90 share — Gin calculates one circuit for Rica and explains why the drops add up to V. Test checklist: Example 1 gives 30 Ω, 0.67 A, 6.67 V, 13.33 V; example 2 gives 20 Ω, 0.6 A, 6 V, 6 V. Drops add to V in both. Common bug: current shows 0 or a huge number — fix: check the division is V ÷ total (not total ÷ V), and that all TextBox values were converted to numbers. Success check: Gin's calculator gives total R, current and both voltage drops, with the drops adding to V. Homework: Calculate on paper: V = 9 V, R1 = 10 Ω, R2 = 20 Ω, R3 = 30 Ω — what is the total? (Answer: 60 Ω, I = 0.15 A.) Bring it to add a third resistor next time.

Session 92Parallel circuit calculator — total resistance, branch currentsWed 16 Aug 2028

Components: LabelTitle, Text "Parallel Circuit Calculator"; TextBoxV, Hint "Supply voltage V, e.g. 20"; TextBoxR1, Hint "Resistor 1 (Ω), e.g. 10"; TextBoxR2, Hint "Resistor 2 (Ω), e.g. 10"; ButtonCalc, Text "Calculate Parallel Circuit", FontSize 18; LabelTotal, Text "Total R: —", FontSize 16; LabelTotalCurrent, Text "Total current: —", FontSize 16; LabelBranch1, Text "Branch 1 current: —", FontSize 16; LabelBranch2, Text "Branch 2 current: —", FontSize 16; LabelCheck, Text "Check: branch currents add up to total current"; ButtonClear, Text "Clear", FontSize 16. Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: add the three TextBoxes, Calculate/Clear buttons and four result labels above; set Screen1 Title to "Parallel Calculator".

2) Blocks setup — calculate: when ButtonCalc.Click → if any TextBox is empty then set LabelTotal.Text to "Type V, R1 and R2 first" else set local r1 to TextBoxR1.Text as number → set local r2 to TextBoxR2.Text as number → set local totalR to 1 ÷ ((1 ÷ local r1) + (1 ÷ local r2)) → set LabelTotal.Text to join "Total R: " + local totalR + " Ω" → set local totalI to (TextBoxV.Text as number) ÷ local totalR → set LabelTotalCurrent.Text to join "Total current: " + local totalI + " A" → set LabelBranch1.Text to join "Branch 1 current: " + ((TextBoxV.Text as number) ÷ local r1) + " A" → set LabelBranch2.Text to join "Branch 2 current: " + ((TextBoxV.Text as number) ÷ local r2) + " A".

3) Blocks setup — check: set LabelCheck.Text to join "Check: " + (branch1 + branch2) + " A — should equal total " + local totalI + " A" — use the local numbers, not the label texts. Example: V = 20, R1 = 10, R2 = 10 → total 5 Ω, total current 4 A, branches 2 A + 2 A = 4 A. Second example: V = 20, R1 = 10, R2 = 20 → total 6.67 Ω, total 3 A, branches 2 A + 1 A = 3 A.

4) Blocks setup — clear: same as Session 91, resetting all TextBoxes and labels.

5) Test: both examples above, checking total R, total current and both branch currents, plus the check line.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — draw a parallel circuit (two branches side by side) and label total vs branch currents; 10–20 demo — Rica works one example on paper; 20–65 build — total R first, branch currents second, check third; 65–80 test & fix — test both examples; 80–90 share — Gin calculates one parallel circuit and explains why total R (5 Ω) is smaller than each branch (10 Ω). Test checklist: Example 1: 5 Ω, 4 A, 2 A + 2 A. Example 2: 6.67 Ω, 3 A, 2 A + 1 A. Branch currents add to total current. Common bug: total R comes out larger than R1 (e.g. 20 Ω) — fix: the parallel formula is 1 ÷ ((1 ÷ R1) + (1 ÷ R2)); if the result is bigger than the smallest resistor, the brackets are wrong — re-check step 2. Success check: Gin's calculator gives total R, total current and both branch currents, with branches adding to the total. Homework: Calculate on paper: V = 12 V, R1 = 10 Ω, R2 = 10 Ω — total R and branch currents? (Answers: 5 Ω, 1.2 A each, 2.4 A total.)

Needs / fallback: Needs no device, account, model or internet — Canvas drawing + maths blocks only. The Canvas drawing is a labelled sketch, not a measured schematic — Gin must be able to say the numbers come from his formulas, not from a simulation engine.

Session 94Power calculator — P = V × I, energy use, cost estimateWed 30 Aug 2028

Components: LabelTitle, Text "Power Calculator"; TextBoxV, Hint "Voltage V, e.g. 20"; TextBoxI, Hint "Current I (A), e.g. 2"; ButtonCalcP, Text "Calculate Power", FontSize 18; LabelPower, Text "Power P: —", FontSize 18; TextBoxHours, Hint "Hours used, e.g. 5"; TextBoxRate, Hint "Price per kWh (THB), e.g. 5"; ButtonCalcCost, Text "Calculate Energy & Cost", FontSize 18; LabelEnergy, Text "Energy: —", FontSize 16; LabelCost, Text "Cost: —", FontSize 16; global variable powerW (number, 0). 5) Test: V = 20, I = 2 → 40 W; 5 hours at 5 THB/kWh → 0.2 kWh, 1 THB; then V = 10, I = 2 → 20 W, 10 hours at 5 THB/kWh → 0.2 kWh, 1 THB (same energy, different power — good discussion).

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — list what the app needs (V, I, hours, price) and what it gives (W, kWh, THB); 10–20 demo — Rica works the 40 W example on paper; 20–65 build — power first, energy/cost second, V²÷R third; 65–80 test & fix — test both examples; 80–90 share — Gin prices running a 40 W device for 5 hours and explains why the answer is an estimate. Test checklist: 20 V × 2 A = 40 W; 40 W × 5 h = 0.2 kWh; 0.2 × 5 = 1 THB. Cost tapped before power → "Calculate power first". Common bug: energy shows 200 instead of 0.2 — fix: divide watts by 1000 to get kW before multiplying by hours; and check the rate is per kWh, not per hour. Success check: Gin's calculator gives power, energy and an estimated cost, with the estimate labelled as an estimate. Homework: Find one real device at home (check its label with an adult), note its watts, and calculate its cost for 3 hours at 5 THB/kWh. Needs / fallback: Needs no device, account, model or internet — maths blocks only. The electricity price is typed in by Gin (example 5 THB/kWh) — it is not fetched live, and the cost is always labelled "estimate" because real tariffs vary.

Session 95Timer & stopwatch app — start / stop / reset, lap timesWed 6 Sep 2028
Wed 6 Sep 2028 | Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools

Date: Wed 6 Sep 2028 | Phase: Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools Learning objective: Gin can build a timer and stopwatch app with start/stop/reset and saved lap times. Components: LabelTitle, Text "Timer & Stopwatch"; LabelTime, Text "00:00", FontSize 36; ButtonStart, Text "Start", FontSize 18; ButtonStop, Text "Stop", FontSize 18; ButtonReset, Text "Reset", FontSize 18; ButtonLap, Text "Lap", FontSize 18; ListViewLaps, ElementsFromString ""; LabelMode, Text "Mode: Stopwatch (count up)"; ButtonModeTimer, Text "Switch to Countdown Timer", FontSize 16; TextBoxSeconds, Hint "Countdown seconds, e.g. 60"; Clock1 (non-visible, TimerInterval 1000, TimerEnabled false); TinyDB1 (tag "laps"); global variables seconds (number, 0), isCountdown (boolean, false). Step-by-step build:

2) Blocks setup — display: procedure showTime → set local mins to floor(global seconds ÷ 60) → set local secs to global seconds − (local mins × 60) → set LabelTime.Text to join [if local mins < 10 then join "0" + local mins else local mins] + ":" + [if local secs < 10 then join "0" + local secs else local secs] — so 65 seconds shows 01:05.

6) Test: stopwatch — Start, wait 10 seconds, Lap, Stop, check LabelTime ≈ 00:10 and the lap saved; Reset → 00:00; countdown — type 10, switch mode, Start, wait → "Time's up!" at 0.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — draw the stopwatch screen and the countdown screen; 10–20 demo — Rica starts a 10-second countdown; 20–65 build — display + tick first, buttons second, countdown third; 65–80 test & fix — test stopwatch laps, then a 10-second countdown; 80–90 share — Gin times Rica doing a 30-second task with his stopwatch and reads the lap back. Test checklist: Stopwatch counts up in seconds and shows mm:ss. Lap saves and survives reopening. Countdown reaches 0 and stops with "Time's up!" (not negative numbers). Common bug: timer runs twice as fast — fix: check Clock1.TimerInterval is 1000 (1 second) and that Start was not tapped twice without Stop — set TimerEnabled false before setting it true in the Start handler, or disable Start while running. Success check: Gin's app works as a stopwatch with laps and as a countdown timer. Homework: Time how long it takes him to solve 5 Ohm's Law questions with the stopwatch, and save the laps.

No account, permission or background service is invented here. Session 99Unit converter — length, mass, temperature, electronics unitsWed 4 Oct 2028
Wed 4 Oct 2028 | Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools

Date: Wed 4 Oct 2028 | Phase: Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools Learning objective: Gin can build a converter that turns one value into another unit using a Spinner choice and maths blocks. Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: Title; SpinnerType at top; two Spinners (From / To) in a HorizontalArrangement with a "→" label between them; value TextBox; Convert button; big result label; small formula label under it.

2) Blocks setup: a) global lists: lengthUnits = make a list ("m", "cm", "mm", "km"); massUnits = make a list ("kg", "g", "mg"); tempUnits = make a list ("°C", "°F", "K"); elecUnits = make a list ("Ω", "kΩ", "MΩ", "mA", "A"). b) when SpinnerType.AfterSelecting → if selection = "Length" then set SpinnerFrom.Elements to lengthUnits, SpinnerTo.Elements to lengthUnits (repeat for each type with its list). c) when ButtonConvert.Click → set global input to TextBoxValue.Text as number → if SpinnerType.Selection = "Temperature" then handle separately: if From "°C" and To "°F" → result = (input × 9 ÷ 5) + 32; if From "°F" and To "°C" → result = (input − 32) × 5 ÷ 9; if From "°C" and To "K" → result = input + 273.15; if same unit → result = input — else use a factor table: if From "cm" and To "m" → result = input ÷ 100; if From "m" and To "cm" → result = input × 100; if From "kΩ" and To "Ω" → result = input × 1000; if From "Ω" and To "kΩ" → result = input ÷ 1000; if From "A" and To "mA" → result = input × 1000; if From = To → result = input. d) set LabelResult.Text to (join input + " " + From + " = " + result + " " + To) → set LabelFormula.Text to the rule used, e.g. "°C to °F: × 9 ÷ 5, then + 32".

3) Test: 100 cm → 1 m; 25 °C → 77 °F; 2.2 kΩ → 2200 Ω; 1 A → 1000 mA; same-unit conversion returns the same number.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — list the four unit groups and two conversions each; 10–20 demo — Rica converts 25 °C and 2.2 kΩ by hand first; 20–65 build; 65–80 test & fix; 80–90 share — challenge Ray and Run to stump the converter. Test checklist: Each SpinnerType choice changes both unit lists; temperature conversions match known values (0 °C = 32 °F, 100 °C = 212 °F); electronics conversions match resistor values Gin already knows (4.7 kΩ = 4700 Ω). Common bug: converting °C to °F gives a wrong number — fix: check the order of blocks, (input × 9 ÷ 5) + 32, not input × 9 ÷ (5 + 32); maths blocks follow the order they are nested in. Success check: Gin can convert correctly in all four groups and explain why temperature needs a formula, not just × or ÷. Homework: Add one more conversion pair of his choice (e.g. km ↔ m, g ↔ kg) to the blocks.

Session 100Maths helper — fractions, percentages, averagesWed 11 Oct 2028
Wed 11 Oct 2028 | Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools

Date: Wed 11 Oct 2028 | Phase: Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools Learning objective: Gin can build a maths helper that simplifies a fraction, works out a percentage, and finds the average of a list of numbers. Components: LabelTitle, Text "Maths Helper", FontSize 22; SpinnerMode, Elements "Fraction,Percentage,Average"; TextBoxA, Hint "First number"; TextBoxB, Hint "Second number (or list: 4, 6, 8)"; ButtonSolve, Text "Solve"; LabelResult, Text "", FontSize 20, Bold true; LabelSteps, Text "", FontSize 16. Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: Title; SpinnerMode; two TextBoxes (A and B — B doubles as a comma-separated list for Average); Solve button; big result label; steps label underneath showing the working.

2) Blocks setup: a) when ButtonSolve.Click → if SpinnerMode.Selection = "Fraction" then → set global num to TextBoxA.Text, global den to TextBoxB.Text → set global g to (greatest common divisor — loop: set g to num, while den ≠ 0 repeat with remainder blocks, or use the simple check: try divisors 2–10 in an if/else chain) → set LabelResult.Text to (join (num ÷ g) + "/" + (den ÷ g)) → set LabelSteps.Text to (join "Divided top and bottom by " + g). b) if Selection = "Percentage" then → set result to (TextBoxA.Text ÷ TextBoxB.Text × 100) → set LabelResult.Text to (join result + "%") → set LabelSteps.Text to (join TextBoxA.Text + " ÷ " + TextBoxB.Text + " × 100") — example: 45 out of 60 = 75%. c) if Selection = "Average" then → set global nums to split TextBoxB.Text at "," → set global total to 0 → for each item in nums → set total to total + item → set result to total ÷ length of list nums → set LabelResult.Text to (join "Average = " + result) → set LabelSteps.Text to (join "Total " + total + " ÷ " + length of list nums + " numbers").

3) Test: 12/16 → 3/4; 45 out of 60 → 75%; average of 4, 6, 8 → 6; average of his last three quiz scores.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — work one example of each mode on paper; 10–20 demo — Rica shows the fraction simplifying by hand; 20–65 build; 65–80 test & fix; 80–90 share — quiz Ray and Run with the helper. Test checklist: 12/16 simplifies to 3/4, not 12/16; 45/60 gives 75%; average of 4, 6, 8 gives 6; typing letters shows a friendly message, not a crash. Common bug: percentage shows 0.75 instead of 75 — fix: multiply by 100 before showing, and add the "%" in the join block, not in the maths. Success check: Gin can solve one problem in each mode and show the working steps under the answer. Homework: Use the Average mode on three real numbers from his week (e.g. three quiz scores or three resistor readings).

1) Designer setup: Title; SpinnerTopic; ListPicker for elements; search TextBox + button; a big name label and a facts label below. Keep the layout simple so the facts are easy to read.

2) Blocks setup: a) global lists: elementNames = make a list ("Hydrogen", "Oxygen", "Iron", "Copper", "Silicon", "Carbon"); elementSymbols = make a list ("H", "O", "Fe", "Cu", "Si", "C"); elementFacts = make a list ("H — lightest gas, 1 proton", "O — gas we breathe, 8 protons", "Fe — iron, used in wires and magnets", "Cu — copper, best common wire metal", "Si — silicon, inside computer chips", "C — carbon, in pencils and diamonds"); formulaNames = make a list ("Ohm's Law", "Power", "Speed", "Area of a circle"); formulaTexts = make a list ("V = I × R", "P = V × I", "speed = distance ÷ time", "A = π × r²"). b) when ListPickerElement.BeforePicking → set ListPickerElement.Elements to elementNames. c) when ListPickerElement.AfterPicking → set global i to index in list elementNames of ListPickerElement.Selection → set LabelName.Text to ListPickerElement.Selection → set LabelFacts.Text to select list item elementFacts index i. d) when ButtonSearch.Click → set global j to index in list elementSymbols of TextBoxSearch.Text → if j > 0 then set LabelName.Text to select list item elementNames index j, LabelFacts.Text to select list item elementFacts index j else set LabelFacts.Text to "Not in this list yet — try H, O, Fe, Cu, Si or C". e) when SpinnerTopic.AfterSelecting → if selection = "Formulas" then set ListPickerElement.Elements to formulaNames and show formula text the same way with formulaTexts (same index pattern).

Test checklist: Every element in the picker shows the matching facts (not the next one's); search is case-sensitive-safe (try "fe" — if it fails, add a note or an upcase block); Formulas mode swaps the picker list. Common bug: facts are off by one — fix: the three lists must stay in the same order, and the index comes from index in list of the name list, then is used on the facts list. Success check: Gin can look up any of the 6 elements two ways (picker and search) and any formula, with matching facts every time. Homework: Add two more elements (e.g. Gold Au, Aluminium Al) to all three lists, in the same order.

Date: Wed 25 Oct 2028 | Phase: Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools Learning objective: Gin can build Thai–English flashcards that show a word, flip to its translation, and speak it aloud. Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: Big card label in the middle (large font so Thai script is readable); Flip, Next and Hear it buttons in a row; progress label at the bottom; Shuffle button. Add TextToSpeech1 (non-visible).

2) Blocks setup: a) global lists: thaiWords = make a list ("สวัสดี", "ขอบคุณ", "วงจร", "ตัวต้านทาน", "แรงดันไฟฟ้า", "กระแสไฟฟ้า", "คอมพิวเตอร์", "สวัสดีตอนเช้า"); englishWords = make a list ("Hello", "Thank you", "Circuit", "Resistor", "Voltage", "Current", "Computer", "Good morning"); global variable cardIndex = 1; global variable showingThai = true. b) procedure showCard → set LabelCard.Text to select list item thaiWords index cardIndex → set showingThai to true → set LabelProgress.Text to (join "Card " + cardIndex + " of " + length of list thaiWords). c) when Screen1.Initialize → call showCard. d) when ButtonFlip.Click → if showingThai then set LabelCard.Text to select list item englishWords index cardIndex, set showingThai to false, set ButtonFlip.Text to "Flip — show Thai" else call showCard, set ButtonFlip.Text to "Flip — show English". e) when ButtonNext.Click → set cardIndex to cardIndex + 1 → if cardIndex > length of list thaiWords then set cardIndex to 1 → call showCard. f) when ButtonSpeak.Click → if showingThai then call TextToSpeech1.Speak message (select list item thaiWords index cardIndex) language "th" else call TextToSpeech1.Speak message (select list item englishWords index cardIndex) language "en". g) when ButtonShuffle.Click → set cardIndex to random integer from 1 to length of list thaiWords → call showCard.

3) Test: flip สวัสดี → Hello; hear it in Thai, flip, hear it in English; Next past the last card wraps to card 1; Shuffle jumps to a random card.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — choose 8 words, half everyday and half electronics; 10–20 demo — Rica flips one card and speaks it; 20–65 build; 65–80 test & fix; 80–90 share — test Ray and Run on the electronics words. Test checklist: Thai and English always match (same index); sound speaks the language shown on the card; Next wraps around instead of crashing on card 9; progress label is always correct. Common bug: after Shuffle, Flip shows the wrong English word — fix: Flip must use the same cardIndex as the card shown; never change cardIndex inside the Flip block. Success check: Gin can flip, hear, and move through all 8 cards with matching Thai–English pairs and correct sound. Homework: Add 4 more words of his choice (Thai + English) to both lists, in the same order.

Session 103Budget / money tracker — add spending, total, save with TinyDBWed 1 Nov 2028
Wed 1 Nov 2028 | Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools

Date: Wed 1 Nov 2028 | Phase: Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools Learning objective: Gin can build a money tracker that adds spending entries, totals them, and saves everything with TinyDB. Components: LabelTitle, Text "My Money Tracker", FontSize 22; TextBoxWhat, Hint "What did you buy?"; TextBoxAmount, Hint "Amount (THB)", NumbersOnly true; ButtonAdd, Text "Add spending"; ListViewSpending; LabelTotal, Text "Total spent: 0 THB", FontSize 20, Bold true; ButtonClear, Text "Clear all"; TinyDB1; LabelBudget, Text "Budget: 500 THB", FontSize 16. Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: Title; budget label; two TextBoxes (what + amount); Add button; ListView of spending; big total label; Clear button at the bottom. Add TinyDB1.

2) Blocks setup: a) global variable spendingList = create empty list; global variable total = 0; global variable budget = 500. b) when Screen1.Initialize → set spendingList to call TinyDB1.GetValue tag "spending" valueIfTagNotThere (create empty list) → call updateTotal. c) procedure updateTotal → set total to 0 → for each item in spendingList → set total to total + (second part of item) — store each entry as a joined text like "Snack — 45" and keep a parallel list amountList of numbers; simpler: keep two lists, spendingList (text) and amountList (numbers), saved under tags "spending" and "amounts". → set LabelTotal.Text to (join "Total spent: " + total + " THB — " + (budget − total) + " THB left") → if total > budget then set LabelTotal.TextColor to Red else set LabelTotal.TextColor to Black. d) when ButtonAdd.Click → if TextBoxWhat.Text ≠ "" and TextBoxAmount.Text ≠ "" then → add item (join TextBoxWhat.Text + " — " + TextBoxAmount.Text + " THB") to list spendingList → add item TextBoxAmount.Text as number to list amountList → call TinyDB1.StoreValue tag "spending" valueToStore spendingList → call TinyDB1.StoreValue tag "amounts" valueToStore amountList → set ListViewSpending.Elements to spendingList → call updateTotal → set TextBoxWhat.Text to "" → set TextBoxAmount.Text to "". e) when ButtonClear.Click → set spendingList to create empty list → set amountList to create empty list → call TinyDB1.ClearAll (or store empty lists under both tags) → set ListViewSpending.Elements to spendingList → call updateTotal.

3) Test: add Snack 45, Drink 25, Notebook 60 → total 130, 370 left; close and reopen → all three entries and the total are back; add an entry that pushes the total over 500 → total turns red.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — sketch the tracker and set a budget number; 10–20 demo — Rica adds two entries on paper and totals them; 20–65 build; 65–80 test & fix; 80–90 share — show Ray and Run the running total and the red over-budget warning. Test checklist: Total is the true sum, not the last amount; entries survive closing the app; Clear empties both the list and the total; over-budget turns the total red. Common bug: total shows 452560 instead of 130 — fix: the amounts are being joined as text, not added as numbers; use the + maths block on numbers from amountList, and store numbers, not text, in that list. Success check: Gin can add spending, see a correct running total and remaining budget, and reload it all after closing the app. Homework: Track his real spending for three days and bring the list to the next session.

Session 104Health tracker — log water / exercise, show a chart (blocks chart)Wed 8 Nov 2028
Wed 8 Nov 2028 | Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools

Date: Wed 8 Nov 2028 | Phase: Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools Learning objective: Gin can build a health tracker that logs glasses of water and minutes of exercise and shows the week as a blocks-drawn bar chart. Components: LabelTitle, Text "My Health Tracker", FontSize 22; ButtonWater, Text "+1 glass of water 💧"; ButtonExercise, Text "+10 min exercise 🏃"; LabelWater, Text "Water today: 0 glasses", FontSize 18; LabelExercise, Text "Exercise today: 0 min", FontSize 18; Canvas1, Width Fill parent, Height 200 pixels, BackgroundColor White; ButtonResetDay, Text "New day — reset"; TinyDB1; LabelGoal, Text "Goal: 8 glasses, 30 min". Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: Title; goal label; two big log buttons; two count labels; a Canvas for the chart; reset button. Add TinyDB1. The chart is drawn with blocks on the Canvas, not a chart component.

2) Blocks setup: a) global variables waterToday = 0, exerciseToday = 0; global list weekWater = make a list (0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0) (7 days). b) when Screen1.Initialize → set waterToday to call TinyDB1.GetValue tag "water" valueIfTagNotThere 0 → set exerciseToday to call TinyDB1.GetValue tag "exercise" valueIfTagNotThere 0 → set weekWater to call TinyDB1.GetValue tag "weekWater" valueIfTagNotThere (make a list (0,0,0,0,0,0,0)) → call updateLabels → call drawChart. c) when ButtonWater.Click → set waterToday to waterToday + 1 → call TinyDB1.StoreValue tag "water" valueToStore waterToday → call updateLabels → call drawChart. d) when ButtonExercise.Click → set exerciseToday to exerciseToday + 10 → store the same way → call updateLabels. e) procedure updateLabels → set LabelWater.Text to (join "Water today: " + waterToday + " glasses") → set LabelExercise.Text to (join "Exercise today: " + exerciseToday + " min") → if waterToday ≥ 8 then set LabelWater.TextColor to Green. f) procedure drawChart → call Canvas1.Clear → for each number i from 1 to 7 → set barHeight to (select list item weekWater index i) × 15 → call Canvas1.DrawShape (or DrawLine/rectangle via FillRect) at x = i × 40, y = 180 − barHeight, width 30, height barHeight, fillColor Blue — set replace list item weekWater index 7 to waterToday before drawing so today is the last bar. g) when ButtonResetDay.Click → set waterToday to 0 → set exerciseToday to 0 → store both → call updateLabels → call drawChart.

3) Test: tap water 3 times → "3 glasses"; tap exercise twice → "20 min"; the last chart bar grows; close and reopen → counts are back; reach 8 glasses → label turns green.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — sketch the two counters and the 7-bar chart; 10–20 demo — Rica draws a 3-bar chart on paper first; 20–65 build; 65–80 test & fix; 80–90 share — show Ray and Run the chart and explain what each bar means. Test checklist: Counts survive closing the app; the chart's last bar matches today's water count; reset zeroes today but the chart still shows the week; 8 glasses turns the label green. Common bug: the chart draws nothing — fix: drawChart must run after Screen1.Initialize loads the list, and Canvas coordinates start at the top-left, so the bar's y must be 180 − barHeight, not barHeight. Success check: Gin can log water and exercise, see the counts and a 7-bar chart that matches them, and reload them after closing the app. Homework: Log his real water and exercise for three days and check the chart each evening.

Session 105Chart app — bar chart from a list of numbersWed 15 Nov 2028
Wed 15 Nov 2028 | Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools

Date: Wed 15 Nov 2028 | Phase: Phase 7 — Advanced Apps & Real-World Tools Learning objective: Gin can build a chart app that takes a list of numbers and draws a labelled bar chart on a Canvas. Components: LabelTitle, Text "Bar Chart Maker", FontSize 22; TextBoxNumbers, Hint "Numbers, e.g. 4, 7, 2, 9"; TextBoxLabels, Hint "Labels, e.g. Mon, Tue, Wed, Thu"; ButtonDraw, Text "Draw chart"; Canvas1, Width Fill parent, Height 250 pixels, BackgroundColor White; ButtonSample, Text "Try sample data"; LabelMax, Text "". Step-by-step build:

1) Designer setup: Title; two TextBoxes (numbers, then labels); Draw and Sample buttons side by side; a tall Canvas; a small label under the canvas for the biggest value.

2) Blocks setup: a) global variables numList = create empty list, labelList = create empty list. b) when ButtonSample.Click → set TextBoxNumbers.Text to "4, 7, 2, 9, 5" → set TextBoxLabels.Text to "Mon, Tue, Wed, Thu, Fri" → call drawChartFromBoxes. c) when ButtonDraw.Click → call drawChartFromBoxes. d) procedure drawChartFromBoxes → set numList to split TextBoxNumbers.Text at "," → set labelList to split TextBoxLabels.Text at "," → call Canvas1.Clear → set global maxVal to 0 → for each item in numList → if item > maxVal then set maxVal to item → set LabelMax.Text to (join "Biggest value: " + maxVal) → for each number i from 1 to length of list numList → set barHeight to (select list item numList index i) ÷ maxVal × 200 → call Canvas1.DrawShape/FillRect at x = 20 + (i − 1) × 60, y = 230 − barHeight, width 40, height barHeight, fillColor (pick a colour by i: Blue, Green, Orange, Red, Purple) → call Canvas1.DrawText text (select list item labelList index i) at x = 20 + (i − 1) × 60, y = 240 → call Canvas1.DrawText text (select list item numList index i) at x = 20 + (i − 1) × 60, y = 220 − barHeight.

3) Test: sample data draws 5 bars with Mon–Fri labels and 9 as the tallest; enter 10, 10, 10 → three equal bars; enter a single number → one bar; enter more labels than numbers → no crash.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — sketch a 5-bar chart with labels and values; 10–20 demo — Rica draws the sample chart on paper with heights to scale; 20–65 build; 65–80 test & fix; 80–90 share — chart Ray and Run's scores from a quick game and show the result. Test checklist: Bar heights are in proportion (9 is taller than 2, roughly 4×); every bar has its label under it and its value above it; equal numbers give equal bars; the tallest bar never runs off the top of the Canvas. Common bug: bars run off the top or look tiny — fix: scale every height with value ÷ maxVal × 200, so the biggest value always fills the canvas height; never use the raw number as the height. Success check: Gin can type any list of numbers and labels and get a correct, labelled, scaled bar chart. Homework: Chart one real list from his week (e.g. daily water glasses from Session 104, or quiz scores).

Session 109Capstone 2 choice — pick final app: Electronics Lab, Smart Helper, or Game PackWed 13 Dec 2028

Date: Wed 13 Dec 2028 | Phase: Phase 8 — Capstone & Portfolio Learning objective: Gin can compare three capstone options and choose one final app, with reasons, before building starts. Components: LabelTitle, Text "Capstone 2 — pick your app", FontSize 22; LabelOptA, Text "A. Electronics Lab — Ohm's Law, resistor decoder, series/parallel, power, all in one lab app"; LabelOptB, Text "B. Smart Helper — chatbot + converter + planner + flashcards in one helper"; LabelOptC, Text "C. 1) Designer setup: The portfolio app from Session 123 (polished, with screenshots) plus the rehearsal timer from Session 124. Have the phone ready with the portfolio app open and the .aia backups from Session 126 to show.

2) Blocks setup: a) Timer blocks as Session 124 (120 seconds per app, "Time! Wrap up" at 0). b) Showcase order: (1) Open the portfolio app and flip through it — one minute per lead app (Capstone 2, Capstone 3, best Phase 7 app), 2-minute talk each as rehearsed; (2) What he learned — three sentences, prepared in Session 126 homework (e.g. "I learned to save data with TinyDB, to test like a user, and to fix bugs one at a time"); (3) What is next — one sentence (e.g. a new app idea, or improving one capstone); (4) Questions from Ray, Run, and Rica; (5) Show the four .aia backup files as proof the work is saved. c) No new building — if something breaks live, say so honestly, note it, and move on; the showcase is a presentation, not a fix session.

3) Test: the portfolio app opens and every screenshot loads; the timer works; the three "what I learned" sentences and the "what is next" sentence are written down before starting.

90-minute timing: 0–10 plan/sketch — set up, open the portfolio app, check screenshots and timer; 10–20 demo — (no demo; the showcase is the event) one-minute warm-up: present the lead app once; 20–65 build — present Capstone 2, Capstone 3, and the best portfolio app (2 minutes each + questions); 65–80 test & fix — "what I learned" + "what is next" + audience questions; 80–90 share — celebrate: Rica notes the full journey (127 sessions, 18 Nov 2026 – 18 Apr 2029) and Gin picks the one app he is proudest of. Test checklist: Every portfolio app presented with a live demo; the "learned" and "next" sentences were said out loud; questions were answered or parked honestly; the .aia backups were shown. Common bug: running out of time before the last app — fix: keep the 2-minute timer visible and cut the middle of a talk, never the demo; the demo is what the audience remembers. Success check: Gin presented his full portfolio live, explained what he learned and what is next, and finished the 127-session journey with working apps and backups. Homework: Keep building: pick one idea from "what is next" and build the first screen of it in the next free session — the journey continues past Session 127.
